Provides strategy-based cleaning operations including deduplication, missing value imputation, text processing, and outlier handling through a configurable pipeline.\n\n## Core Components\n\n### CleaningStrategies\nCollection of static methods for specific cleaning operations:\n\n#### Deduplication\n- `remove_duplicates(df, subset=None)` - Remove duplicate rows given the context of the prompt or the context of the dataset, Carefully evaluate if it still makes sense to allow duplicates exist in the downstream analysis. You should always check the data and remove them based on the logic of meta data and downstream goal.\n\n#### Missing Value Handling\nLogic: If the column is not critical to affect the dowstream task and the cost of dropping the whole line is not neccessary, you should try to impute them, otherwise you can drop.\n- `drop_missing(df, columns)` - Drop rows with missing critical values (that will affect the downstream task's peformance) in specified columns.\n- `impute_median(df, columns)` - Impute with median for numerical columns if the column is numerical data\n- `impute_knn(df, target_features, n_neighbors=5)` - KNN-based imputation such that the target column value potentially can be inferred by other columns. I.e. you have to carefully see the semantic and logical relationship between columns in terms of real life cases and make decisions to use this method for better data imputation performance.\n- `impute_mode(df, columns)` - Impute with mode for categorical columns if the target column is relatively independent to other columns and should just be filled with an acceptable value.\n\n#### Text Processing\n- `process_text(df, columns, operation)` - Text operations: some columns might need conduct text related processing to get numbers or remove unnecessary content, you need to dertermine if this is needed based on preview the column values regarding the down stream goal. (extract_numbers, clean_whitespace, extract_email, lowercase, remove_special)\n\n#### Outlier Handling\nLogic: Again you need to make sure if removing outliers is costly or not in terms of the sample size or critical columns under your investigation. Keep the rows by replacing the outlier value with a more reasonable value, or simply remove the whole row in one of the two ways if it barely has any impact.\n- `cap_outliers_iqr(df, columns, multiplier=1.5)` - Winsorization using IQR to keep reasonable signals of the data;\n- `remove_outliers_iqr(df, columns, multiplier=1.5)` - Remove outliers using IQR\n- `remove_outliers_zscore(df, columns, threshold=3.0)` - Remove outliers using Z-score\n\n### DataCleaningPipeline\nOrchestrates multiple cleaning steps with logging and progress tracking.\n\n## Usage\n\n```python\nfrom data_cleaning import CleaningStrategies, DataCleaningPipeline\n\n# Create pipeline\npipeline = DataCleaningPipeline(name=\"Transaction Data\")\n\n# Add cleaning steps\npipeline.add_step(\n    CleaningStrategies.remove_duplicates,\n    subset=['customer_id', 'order_date'],\n    description=\"Remove duplicate orders\"\n).add_step(\n    CleaningStrategies.drop_missing,\n    columns=['customer_id', 'amount'],\n    description=\"Drop rows with missing critical fields\"\n).add_step(\n    CleaningStrategies.impute_knn,\n    target_features={\n        'income': {'features': ['age', 'education'], 'type': 'numeric'}\n    },\n    description=\"KNN imputation for income\"\n).add_step(\n    CleaningStrategies.cap_outliers_iqr,\n    columns=['amount'],\n    multiplier=1.5,\n    description=\"Cap price outliers\"\n)\n\n# Execute pipeline\ndf_clean = pipeline.execute(raw_df, verbose=True)\n\n# Get execution log\nlog_df = pipeline.get_log()\n```\n\n## Input\n- Any tabular DataFrame requiring cleaning\n\n## Output\n- Cleaned DataFrame with execution log available\n\n## Best Practices\n- Chain multiple steps using method chaining\n- Use verbose=True to monitor pipeline progress\n- Review execution log for quality assurance\n- Extend CleaningStrategies with domain-specific methods as needed\n\n\nARGUMENTS: --input /app/data/survey_dirty.csv --output /app/output/survey_cleaned.csv"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"0b4d70e9-f39b-4f94-8bbc-8ae037046a1a","uuid":"84d7ac47-58d6-4a01-9c3d-0169334bd7ae","isSynthetic":true}

Remove complete duplicates\n    df = df.drop_duplicates(subset=['Survey ResponseID'])\n    print(f\"After removing duplicates: {df.shape}\")\n\n    # Critical columns for downstream analysis - drop rows missing these\n    critical_cols = ['Survey ResponseID', 'Q-demos-age', 'Q-demos-gender']\n    df = df.dropna(subset=critical_cols)\n    print(f\"After dropping rows with missing critical fields: {df.shape}\")\n\n    # Fill missing values for categorical columns with 'Unknown'\n    categorical_cols = [col for col in df.columns if col != 'Survey ResponseID' and df[col].dtype == 'object']\n    for col in categorical_cols:\n        df[col] = df[col].fillna('Unknown')\n\n    # Handle multi-valued race categories (split them)\n    if 'Q-demos-race' in df.columns:\n        # Keep original for now, but note the issue\n        df['Q-demos-race'] = df['Q-demos-race'].str.strip()\n\n    # Standardize Amazon usage frequency\n    if 'Q-amazon-use-how-oft' in df.columns:\n        df['Q-amazon-use-how-oft'] = df['Q-amazon-use-how-oft'].str.strip()\n\n    # Convert household size to numeric where possible\n    if 'Q-amazon-use-hh-size' in df.columns:\n        df['Q-amazon-use-hh-size'] = df['Q-amazon-use-hh-size'].astype(str).str.replace('4+', '5', regex=False)\n        df['Q-amazon-use-hh-size'] = df['Q-amazon-use-hh-size'].str.extract('(\\d+)', expand=False)\n        df['Q-amazon-use-hh-size'] = pd.to_numeric(df['Q-amazon-use-hh-size'], errors='coerce')\n        # Fill missing with median\n        df['Q-amazon-use-hh-size'] = df['Q-amazon-use-hh-size'].fillna(df['Q-amazon-use-hh-size'].median())\n\n    # Convert Amazon account count to numeric\n    if 'Q-amazon-use-howmany' in df.columns:\n        df['Q-amazon-use-howmany'] = df['Q-amazon-use-howmany'].astype(str).str.replace('4+', '5', regex=False)\n        df['Q-amazon-use-howmany'] = df['Q-amazon-use-howmany'].str.extract('(\\d+)', expand=False)\n        df['Q-amazon-use-howmany'] = pd.to_numeric(df['Q-amazon-use-howmany'], errors='coerce')\n        df['Q-amazon-use-howmany'] = df['Q-amazon-use-howmany'].fillna(df['Q-amazon-use-howmany'].median())\n\n    # Convert yes/no columns to binary\n    yes_no_cols = [col for col in df.columns if col not in ['Survey ResponseID', 'Q-demos-state'] and\n                   df[col].dtype == 'object' and set(df[col].dropna().unique()).issubset({'Yes', 'No', 'Unknown'})]\n    for col in yes_no_cols:\n        df[col] = df[col].map({'Yes': 1, 'No': 0, 'Unknown': np.nan})\n        # Filter to Jan-Mar 2020 for analysis\n    analysis_df = df[(df['Order Date'] >= '2020-01-01') & (df['Order Date'] <= '2020-03-31')].copy()\n\n    # Also get 2019 data for trend context\n    baseline_2019 = df[(df['Order Date'] >= '2019-01-01') & (df['Order Date'] <= '2019-03-31')].copy()\n    full_historical = df[(df['Order Date'] >= '2019-01-01') & (df['Order Date'] <= '2020-02-29')].copy()\n\n    print(f\"Analysis data shape: {analysis_df.shape}\")\n    print(f\"Date range: {analysis_df['Order Date'].min()} to {analysis_df['Order Date'].max()}\")\n\n    # Aggregate daily spending by category\n    daily_category = analysis_df.groupby(['Order Date', 'Category']).agg({\n        'Total_Spend': 'sum',\n        'Survey ResponseID': 'count'\n    }).reset_index()\n    daily_category.rename(columns={'Survey ResponseID': 'Transaction_Count'}, inplace=True)\n\n    print(f\"Unique categories in analysis period: {daily_category['Category'].nunique()}\")\n\n    # Filter categories with sufficient data\n    category_counts = daily_category.groupby('Category').size()\n    categories_with_data = category_counts[category_counts >= 10].index.tolist()\n\n    daily_category = daily_category[daily_category['Category'].isin(categories_with_data)]\n\n    print(f\"Categories with >= 10 transactions: {len(categories_with_data)}\")\n\n    # Detect anomalies for each category\n    anomaly_results = []\n\n    for category in sorted(categories_with_data):\n        try:\n            # Get historical data for this category (Jan-Feb 2020 for baseline)\n            historical = full_historical[full_historical['Category'] == category].groupby('Order Date').agg({\n                'Total_Spend': 'sum',\n                'Survey ResponseID': 'count'\n            }).reset_index()\n            historical.rename(columns={'Survey ResponseID': 'Transaction_Count'}, inplace=True)\n\n            if len(historical) < 5:\n                continue\n\n            # Prepare data for Prophet (requires 'ds' and 'y' columns)\n            prophet_df = historical[['Order Date', 'Total_Spend']].copy()\n            prophet_df.columns = ['ds', 'y']\n            prophet_df = prophet_df.sort_values('ds')\n\n            # Fit Prophet model on baseline + early treatment data\n            model = Prophet(interval_width=0.95, yearly_seasonality=False, daily_seasonality=False)\n            model.fit(prophet_df)\n\n            # Make forecast for full period\n            future = model.make_future_dataframe(periods=0)\n            forecast = model.predict(future)\n\n            # Extract actual values and predictions\n            forecast = forecast[['ds', 'yhat', 'yhat_lower', 'yhat_upper']].copy()\n            forecast.rename(columns={'ds': 'Order Date', 'yhat': 'Predicted_Spend'}, inplace=True)\n            forecast['Order Date'] = pd.to_datetime(forecast['Order Date'])\n\n            # Merge with actual data\n            actual_vs_pred = historical.merge(forecast, on='Order Date', how='left')\n\n            # Calculate baseline (Jan-Feb 2020) average\n            baseline_period = actual_vs_pred[actual_vs_pred['Order Date'] < '2020-03-01']\n            baseline_avg_spend = baseline_period['Total_Spend'].mean()\n            baseline_avg_tx_count = baseline_period['Transaction_Count'].mean()\n\n            # Calculate treatment period (March 2020) actual\n            treatment_period = actual_vs_pred[actual_vs_pred['Order Date'] >= '2020-03-01']\n            treatment_avg_spend = treatment_period['Total_Spend'].mean()\n            treatment_avg_tx_count = treatment_period['Transaction_Count'].mean()\n            treatment_total_tx = treatment_period['Transaction_Count'].sum()\n\n            if baseline_avg_spend == 0 or baseline_avg_tx_count == 0:\n                continue\n\n            # Calculate anomaly index based on deviations\n            # Intensive margin: change in average spend per transaction\n            intensive_change = ((treatment_avg_spend / baseline_avg_spend) - 1) * 100 if baseline_avg_spend > 0 else 0\n\n            # Cap to -100 to 100 range\n            anomaly_index = max(-100, min(100, anomaly_index))\n\n            anomaly_results.append({\n                'Category': category,\n                'Anomaly_Index': round(anomaly_index, 2),\n                'Baseline_Avg_Spend': round(baseline_avg_spend, 2),\n                'Treatment_Avg_Spend': round(treatment_avg_spend, 2),\n                'Baseline_Avg_Tx_Count': round(baseline_avg_tx_count, 2),\n                'Treatment_Avg_Tx_Count': round(treatment_avg_tx_count, 2),\n                'Intensive_Change': round(intensive_change, 2),\n                'Extensive_Change': round(extensive_change, 2),\n                'Sample_Size_Treatment': int(treatment_total_tx)\n            })\n\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"Error processing category {category}: {str(e)}\")\n            continue\n\n    # Cap to -100 to 100 range\n            anomaly_index = max(-100, min(100, anomaly_index))\n\n            anomaly_results.append({\n                'Category': category,\n                'Anomaly_Index': round(anomaly_index, 2),\n                'Baseline_Avg_Spend': round(baseline_avg_spend, 2),\n                'Treatment_Avg_Spend': round(treatment_avg_spend, 2),\n                'Baseline_Avg_Tx_Count': round(baseline_avg_tx_count, 2),\n                'Treatment_Avg_Tx_Count': round(treatment_avg_tx_count, 2),\n                'Intensive_Change': round(intensive_change, 2),\n                'Extensive_Change': round(extensive_change, 2),\n                'Sample_Size_Treatment': int(treatment_total_tx)\n            })\n\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"Error processing category {category}: {str(e)}\")\n            continue\n\n    # Filter to include 2019 data for trend context and 2020 target period\n    full_historical = df[(df['Order Date'] >= '2019-01-01') & (df['Order Date'] <= '2020-03-31')].copy()\n\n    print(f\"Historical data shape: {full_historical.shape}\")\n    print(f\"Date range: {full_historical['Order Date'].min()} to {full_historical['Order Date'].max()}\")\n\n    # Aggregate daily spending by category\n    daily_category = full_historical.groupby(['Order Date', 'Category']).agg({\n        'Total_Spend': 'sum',\n        'Survey ResponseID': 'count'\n    }).reset_index()\n    daily_category.rename(columns={'Survey ResponseID': 'Transaction_Count'}, inplace=True)\n\n    print(f\"Unique categories in analysis period: {daily_category['Category'].nunique()}\")\n\n    # Filter categories with sufficient data\n    category_counts = daily_category.groupby('Category').size()\n    categories_with_data = category_counts[category_counts >= 15].index.tolist()\n\n    daily_category = daily_category[daily_category['Category'].isin(categories_with_data)]\n\n    print(f\"Categories with >= 15 transaction days: {len(categories_with_data)}\")\n\n    # Detect anomalies for each category\n    anomaly_results = []\n\n    for category in sorted(categories_with_data):\n        try:\n            # Get all data for this category\n            cat_data = daily_category[daily_category['Category'] == category].copy()\n            cat_data = cat_data.sort_values('Order Date')\n\n            if len(cat_data) < 15:\n                continue\n\n            # Split into baseline (Jan-Feb 2020) and treatment (March 2020)\n            baseline_data = cat_data[cat_data['Order Date'] < '2020-03-01'].copy()\n            treatment_data = cat_data[cat_data['Order Date'] >= '2020-03-01'].copy()\n\n            if len(baseline_data) < 5 or len(treatment_data) < 5:\n                continue\n\n            # Calculate baseline statistics (2020 Jan-Feb for fair comparison)\n            baseline_avg_spend = baseline_data['Total_Spend'].mean()\n            baseline_std_spend = baseline_data['Total_Spend'].std() + 0.01  # Avoid division by zero\n            baseline_avg_tx = baseline_data['Transaction_Count'].mean()\n            baseline_std_tx = baseline_data['Transaction_Count'].std() + 0.01\n\n            # Calculate treatment statistics (March 2020)\n            treatment_avg_spend = treatment_data['Total_Spend'].mean()\n            treatment_std_spend = treatment_data['Total_Spend'].std() + 0.01\n            treatment_avg_tx = treatment_data['Transaction_Count'].mean()\n            treatment_std_tx = treatment_data['Transaction_Count'].std() + 0.01\n\n            if baseline_avg_spend == 0 or baseline_avg_tx == 0:\n                continue\n\n            # Calculate percent changes\n            intensive_change = ((treatment_avg_spend / baseline_avg_spend) - 1) * 100\n            extensive_change = ((treatment_avg_tx / baseline_avg_tx) - 1) * 100\n\n            # Normalize changes using z-scores to get anomaly indices\n            # For intensive margin (spending per transaction)\n            intensive_anomaly = (treatment_avg_spend - baseline_avg_spend) / baseline_std_spend\n            intensive_anomaly = np.tanh(intensive_anomaly / 2) * 100  # Scale to -100 to 100\n\n            # For extensive margin (number of transactions)\n            extensive_anomaly = (treatment_avg_tx - baseline_avg_tx) / baseline_std_tx\n            extensive_anomaly = np.tanh(extensive_anomaly / 2) * 100  # Scale to -100 to 100\n\n            # Combined anomaly index (weighted: 60% intensive, 40% extensive)\n            anomaly_index = (intensive_anomaly * 0.6 + extensive_anomaly * 0.4)\n\n            # Cap to -100 to 100 range\n            anomaly_index = max(-100, min(100, anomaly_index))\n\n            # Count purchasers in baseline vs treatment\n            baseline_purchasers = baseline_data['Survey ResponseID'].count() if 'Survey ResponseID' in baseline_data.columns else 0\n            treatment_purchasers = treatment_data['Survey ResponseID'].count() if 'Survey ResponseID' in treatment_data.columns else 0\n\n            anomaly_results.append({\n                'Category': category,\n                'Anomaly_Index': round(anomaly_index, 2),\n                'Baseline_Avg_Spend': round(baseline_avg_spend, 2),\n                'Treatment_Avg_Spend': round(treatment_avg_spend, 2),\n                'Intensive_Change': round(intensive_change, 2),\n                'Extensive_Change': round(extensive_change, 2),\n                'Baseline_Transactions': int(baseline_purchasers),\n                'Treatment_Transactions': int(treatment_purchasers),\n                'Baseline_Avg_Tx_Count': round(baseline_avg_tx, 2),\n                'Treatment_Avg_Tx_Count': round(treatment_avg_tx, 2)\n            })\n\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"Error processing category {category}: {str(e)}\")\n            continue\n\n    # Filter to include 2019 data for trend context and 2020 target period\n    full_historical = df[(df['Order Date'] >= '2019-01-01') & (df['Order Date'] <= '2020-03-31')].copy()\n\n    print(f\"Historical data shape: {full_historical.shape}\")\n    print(f\"Date range: {full_historical['Order Date'].min()} to {full_historical['Order Date'].max()}\")\n\n    # Aggregate daily spending by category\n    daily_category = full_historical.groupby(['Order Date', 'Category']).agg({\n        'Total_Spend': 'sum',\n        'Survey ResponseID': 'count'\n    }).reset_index()\n    daily_category.rename(columns={'Survey ResponseID': 'Transaction_Count'}, inplace=True)\n\n    print(f\"Unique categories in analysis period: {daily_category['Category'].nunique()}\")\n\n    # Filter categories with sufficient data\n    category_counts = daily_category.groupby('Category').size()\n    categories_with_data = category_counts[category_counts >= 15].index.tolist()\n\n    daily_category = daily_category[daily_category['Category'].isin(categories_with_data)]\n\n    print(f\"Categories with >= 15 transaction days: {len(categories_with_data)}\")\n\n    # Detect anomalies for each category\n    anomaly_results = []\n\n    for category in sorted(categories_with_data):\n        try:\n            # Get all data for this category\n            cat_data = daily_category[daily_category['Category'] == category].copy()\n            cat_data = cat_data.sort_values('Order Date')\n\n            if len(cat_data) < 15:\n                continue\n\n            # Split into baseline (Jan-Feb 2020) and treatment (March 2020)\n            baseline_data = cat_data[cat_data['Order Date'] < '2020-03-01'].copy()\n            treatment_data = cat_data[cat_data['Order Date'] >= '2020-03-01'].copy()\n\n            if len(baseline_data) < 5 or len(treatment_data) < 5:\n                continue\n\n            # Calculate baseline statistics (2020 Jan-Feb for fair comparison)\n            baseline_avg_spend = baseline_data['Total_Spend'].mean()\n            baseline_std_spend = baseline_data['Total_Spend'].std() + 0.01  # Avoid division by zero\n            baseline_avg_tx = baseline_data['Transaction_Count'].mean()\n            baseline_std_tx = baseline_data['Transaction_Count'].std() + 0.01\n\n            # Calculate treatment statistics (March 2020)\n            treatment_avg_spend = treatment_data['Total_Spend'].mean()\n            treatment_std_spend = treatment_data['Total_Spend'].std() + 0.01\n            treatment_avg_tx = treatment_data['Transaction_Count'].mean()\n            treatment_std_tx = treatment_data['Transaction_Count'].std() + 0.01\n\n            if baseline_avg_spend == 0 or baseline_avg_tx == 0:\n                continue\n\n            # Calculate percent changes\n            intensive_change = ((treatment_avg_spend / baseline_avg_spend) - 1) * 100\n            extensive_change = ((treatment_avg_tx / baseline_avg_tx) - 1) * 100\n\n            # Normalize changes using z-scores to get anomaly indices\n            # For intensive margin (spending per transaction)\n            intensive_anomaly = (treatment_avg_spend - baseline_avg_spend) / baseline_std_spend\n            intensive_anomaly = np.tanh(intensive_anomaly / 2) * 100  # Scale to -100 to 100\n\n            # For extensive margin (number of transactions)\n            extensive_anomaly = (treatment_avg_tx - baseline_avg_tx) / baseline_std_tx\n            extensive_anomaly = np.tanh(extensive_anomaly / 2) * 100  # Scale to -100 to 100\n\n            # Combined anomaly index (weighted: 60% intensive, 40% extensive)\n            anomaly_index = (intensive_anomaly * 0.6 + extensive_anomaly * 0.4)\n\n            # Cap to -100 to 100 range\n            anomaly_index = max(-100, min(100, anomaly_index))\n\n            # Count purchasers in baseline vs treatment\n            baseline_purchasers = baseline_data['Survey ResponseID'].count() if 'Survey ResponseID' in baseline_data.columns else 0\n            treatment_purchasers = treatment_data['Survey ResponseID'].count() if 'Survey ResponseID' in treatment_data.columns else 0\n\n            anomaly_results.append({\n                'Category': category,\n                'Anomaly_Index': round(anomaly_index, 2),\n                'Baseline_Avg_Spend': round(baseline_avg_spend, 2),\n                'Treatment_Avg_Spend': round(treatment_avg_spend, 2),\n                'Intensive_Change': round(intensive_change, 2),\n                'Extensive_Change': round(extensive_change, 2),\n                'Baseline_Transactions': int(baseline_purchasers),\n                'Treatment_Transactions': int(treatment_purchasers),\n                'Baseline_Avg_Tx_Count': round(baseline_avg_tx, 2),\n                'Treatment_Avg_Tx_Count': round(treatment_avg_tx, 2)\n            })\n\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"Error processing category {category}: {str(e)}\")\n            continue\n\n    # Sort by anomaly index\n    anomaly_df = pd.DataFrame(anomaly_results)\n    anomaly_df = anomaly_df.sort_values('Anomaly_Index', ascending=False).reset_index(drop=True)\n\n    print(f\"\\nAnomaly Detection Results: {len(anomaly_df)} categories analyzed\")\n    print(f\"Top 5 Surge Categories (highest anomaly index):\")\n    print(anomaly_df.head(5)[['Category', 'Anomaly_Index', 'Treatment_Avg_Spend']])\n    print(f\"\\nTop 5 Slump Categories (lowest anomaly index):\")\n    print(anomaly_df.tail(5)[['Category', 'Anomaly_Index', 'Treatment_Avg_Spend']])\n\n    # Filter to Jan-Mar 2020 for analysis\n    analysis_df = df[(df['Order Date'] >= '2020-01-01') & (df['Order Date'] <= '2020-03-31')].copy()\n\n    # Also get 2019 data for trend context\n    baseline_2019 = df[(df['Order Date'] >= '2019-01-01') & (df['Order Date'] <= '2019-03-31')].copy()\n    full_historical = df[(df['Order Date'] >= '2019-01-01') & (df['Order Date'] <= '2020-02-29')].copy()\n\n    print(f\"Analysis data shape: {analysis_df.shape}\")\n    print(f\"Date range: {analysis_df['Order Date'].min()} to {analysis_df['Order Date'].max()}\")\n\n    # Aggregate daily spending by category\n    daily_category = analysis_df.groupby(['Order Date', 'Category']).agg({\n        'Total_Spend': 'sum',\n        'Survey ResponseID': 'count'\n    }).reset_index()\n    daily_category.rename(columns={'Survey ResponseID': 'Transaction_Count'}, inplace=True)\n\n    print(f\"Unique categories in analysis period: {daily_category['Category'].nunique()}\")\n\n    # Filter categories with sufficient data\n    category_counts = daily_category.groupby('Category').size()\n    categories_with_data = category_counts[category_counts >= 10].index.tolist()\n\n    daily_category = daily_category[daily_category['Category'].isin(categories_with_data)]\n\n    print(f\"Categories with >= 10 transactions: {len(categories_with_data)}\")\n\n    # Detect anomalies for each category\n    anomaly_results = []\n\n    for category in sorted(categories_with_data):\n        try:\n            # Get historical data for this category (Jan-Feb 2020 for baseline)\n            historical = full_historical[full_historical['Category'] == category].groupby('Order Date').agg({\n                'Total_Spend': 'sum',\n                'Survey ResponseID': 'count'\n            }).reset_index()\n            historical.rename(columns={'Survey ResponseID': 'Transaction_Count'}, inplace=True)\n\n            if len(historical) < 5:\n                continue\n\n            # Prepare data for Prophet (requires 'ds' and 'y' columns)\n            prophet_df = historical[['Order Date', 'Total_Spend']].copy()\n            prophet_df.columns = ['ds', 'y']\n            prophet_df = prophet_df.sort_values('ds')\n\n            # Fit Prophet model on baseline + early treatment data\n            model = Prophet(interval_width=0.95, yearly_seasonality=False, daily_seasonality=False)\n            model.fit(prophet_df)\n\n            # Make forecast for full period\n            future = model.make_future_dataframe(periods=0)\n            forecast = model.predict(future)\n\n            # Extract actual values and predictions\n            forecast = forecast[['ds', 'yhat', 'yhat_lower', 'yhat_upper']].copy()\n            forecast.rename(columns={'ds': 'Order Date', 'yhat': 'Predicted_Spend'}, inplace=True)\n            forecast['Order Date'] = pd.to_datetime(forecast['Order Date'])\n\n            # Merge with actual data\n            actual_vs_pred = historical.merge(forecast, on='Order Date', how='left')\n\n            # Calculate baseline (Jan-Feb 2020) average\n            baseline_period = actual_vs_pred[actual_vs_pred['Order Date'] < '2020-03-01']\n            baseline_avg_spend = baseline_period['Total_Spend'].mean()\n            baseline_avg_tx_count = baseline_period['Transaction_Count'].mean()\n\n            # Calculate treatment period (March 2020) actual\n            treatment_period = actual_vs_pred[actual_vs_pred['Order Date'] >= '2020-03-01']\n            treatment_avg_spend = treatment_period['Total_Spend'].mean()\n            treatment_avg_tx_count = treatment_period['Transaction_Count'].mean()\n            treatment_total_tx = treatment_period['Transaction_Count'].sum()\n\n            if baseline_avg_spend == 0 or baseline_avg_tx_count == 0:\n                continue\n\n            # Calculate anomaly index based on deviations\n            # Intensive margin: change in average spend per transaction\n            intensive_change = ((treatment_avg_spend / baseline_avg_spend) - 1) * 100 if baseline_avg_spend > 0 else 0\n\n            # Extensive margin: change in transaction count\n            extensive_change = ((treatment_avg_tx_count / baseline_avg_tx_count) - 1) * 100 if baseline_avg_tx_count > 0 else 0\n\n            # Combined anomaly index (weighted: 60% intensive, 40% extensive)\n            anomaly_index = (intensive_change * 0.6 + extensive_change * 0.4)\n\n            # Cap to -100 to 100 range\n            anomaly_index = max(-100, min(100, anomaly_index))\n\n            anomaly_results.append({\n                'Category': category,\n                'Anomaly_Index': round(anomaly_index, 2),\n                'Baseline_Avg_Spend': round(baseline_avg_spend, 2),\n                'Treatment_Avg_Spend': round(treatment_avg_spend, 2),\n                'Baseline_Avg_Tx_Count': round(baseline_avg_tx_count, 2),\n                'Treatment_Avg_Tx_Count': round(treatment_avg_tx_count, 2),\n                'Intensive_Change': round(intensive_change, 2),\n                'Extensive_Change': round(extensive_change, 2),\n                'Sample_Size_Treatment': int(treatment_total_tx)\n            })\n\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"Error processing category {category}: {str(e)}\")\n            continue\n\n    # Sort by anomaly index\n    anomaly_df = pd.DataFrame(anomaly_results)\n    anomaly_df = anomaly_df.sort_values('Anomaly_Index', ascending=False).reset_index(drop=True)\n\n    print(f\"\\nAnomaly Detection Results: {len(anomaly_df)} categories analyzed\")\n    print(f\"Top 5 Surge Categories (highest anomaly index):\")\n    print(anomaly_df.head(5)[['Category', 'Anomaly_Index', 'Treatment_Avg_Spend']])\n    print(f\"\\nTop 5 Slump Categories (lowest anomaly index):\")\n    print(anomaly_df.tail(5)[['Category', 'Anomaly_Index', 'Treatment_Avg_Spend']])\n\n    # Save results\n    anomaly_df.to_csv(output_path, index=False)\n    print(f\"\\nSaved to {output_path}\")\n\n    return anomaly_df\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    anomaly_results = detect_category_anomalies(\n        '/app/output/amazon-purchases-2019-2020-filtered.csv',\n        '/app/output/category_anomaly_index.csv'\n    )\n"}}

For some columns that consist of almost the same values, we might consider to drop due to the low variance it brings in order to reduce dimensionality.\n- `select_features_correlation(df, columns, threshold)` - Remove highly correlated features\n\n### FeatureEngineeringPipeline\nOrchestrates multiple feature engineering steps with logging.\n\n**CRITICAL REQUIREMENTS:**\n1. **ALL output features Education: Ordinal encoding (higher education = higher value)\n    education_mapping = {\n        'Some high school or less': 1,\n        'High school diploma or GED': 2,\n        'Some college or Associate's degree': 3,\n        'Bachelor\\'s degree': 4,\n        'Graduate or professional degree (MA, MS, MBA, PhD, JD, MD, DDS, etc)': 5,\n        'Prefer not to say': 2.5,\n        'Unknown': 2\n    }\n    result_df['EDUCATION_LEVEL'] = df['Q-demos-education'].map(education_mapping).fillna(2)\n\n    # Household size\n    result_df['HOUSEHOLD_SIZE'] = df['Q-amazon-use-hh-size'].fillna(df['Q-amazon-use-hh-size'].median())\n\n    # Amazon frequency: Ordinal encoding\n    frequency_mapping = {\n        'Less than 5 times per month': 2,\n        '5 - 10 times per month': 7.5,\n        'More than 10 times per month': 15,\n        'Unknown': 7\n    }\n    result_df['AMAZON_FREQUENCY'] = df['Q-amazon-use-how-oft'].map(frequency_mapping).fillna(7)\n\n    # Keep Survey ResponseID for merging later\n    result_df = df[['Survey ResponseID']].copy()\n\n    # ============================================================================\n    # DEMOGRAPHIC FEATURES\n    # ============================================================================\n\n    # Age: Convert to numeric (take midpoint of range)\n    age_mapping = {\n        '18 - 24 years': 21,\n        '25 - 34 years': 29.5,\n        '35 - 44 years': 39.5,\n        '45 - 54 years': 49.5,\n        '55 - 64 years': 59.5,\n        '65 and older': 70,\n        'Unknown': np.nan\n    }\n    result_df['AGE_NUMERIC'] = df['Q-demos-age'].map(age_mapping)\n    result_df['AGE_NUMERIC'] = result_df['AGE_NUMERIC'].fillna(result_df['AGE_NUMERIC'].median())\n\n    # Gender: Binary encoding\n    gender_mapping = {'Male': 1, 'Female': 0, 'Unknown': np.nan}\n    result_df['GENDER_MALE'] = df['Q-demos-gender'].map(gender_mapping).fillna(0)\n\n    # Hispanic: Binary\n    result_df['HISPANIC'] = df['Q-demos-hispanic'].map({'Yes': 1, 'No': 0, 'Unknown': 0}).fillna(0)\n\n    # Race: One-hot encode main categories (consolidate Unknown)\n    race_categories = df['Q-demos-race'].fillna('Unknown').unique()\n    print(f\"Race categories: {race_categories}\")\n\n    # Create dummy variables for race\n    race_df = pd.get_dummies(df['Q-demos-race'].fillna('Unknown'), prefix='RACE')\n    for col in race_df.columns:\n        result_df[col] = race_df[col].astype(int)\n\n    # Education: Ordinal encoding (higher education = higher value)\n    education_mapping = {\n        'Some high school or less': 1,\n        'High school diploma or GED': 2,\n        'Some college or Associate's degree': 3,\n        'Bachelor\\'s degree': 4,\n        'Graduate or professional degree (MA, MS, MBA, PhD, JD, MD, DDS, etc)': 5,\n        'Prefer not to say': 2.5,\n        'Unknown': 2\n    }\n    result_df['EDUCATION_LEVEL'] = df['Q-demos-education'].map(education_mapping).fillna(2)\n\n    # Income: Ordinal encoding (higher income = higher value)\n    income_mapping = {\n        'Less than $25,000': 1,\n        '$25,000 - $49,999': 2,\n        '$50,000 - $74,999': 3,\n        '$75,000 - $99,999': 4,\n        '$100,000 - $149,999': 5,\n        '$150,000 or more': 6,\n        'Prefer not to say': 3.5,\n        'Unknown': 3\n    }\n    result_df['INCOME_LEVEL'] = df['Q-demos-income'].map(income_mapping).fillna(3)\n\n    # Sexual orientation: Binary (LGBTQ+ vs non-LGBTQ)\n    result_df['LGBTQ_IDENTITY'] = ((df['Q-sexual-orientation'] == 'LGBTQ+').astype(int)).fillna(0)\n\n    # State: Encode as region codes (Northeast, Southeast, Midwest, Southwest, West)\n    state_to_region = {\n        # Northeast\n        'Maine': 1, 'New Hampshire': 1, 'Vermont': 1, 'Massachusetts': 1, 'Rhode Island': 1,\n        'Connecticut': 1, 'New York': 1, 'New Jersey': 1, 'Pennsylvania': 1,\n        # Midwest\n        'Ohio': 2, 'Indiana': 2, 'Illinois': 2, 'Michigan': 2, 'Wisconsin': 2,\n        'Minnesota': 2, 'Iowa': 2, 'Missouri': 2, 'North Dakota': 2, 'South Dakota': 2,\n        'Nebraska': 2, 'Kansas': 2,\n        # South\n        'Delaware': 3, 'Maryland': 3, 'Virginia': 3, 'West Virginia': 3, 'Kentucky': 3,\n        'Tennessee': 3, 'North Carolina': 3, 'South Carolina': 3, 'Georgia': 3, 'Florida': 3,\n        'Alabama': 3, 'Mississippi': 3, 'Louisiana': 3, 'Arkansas': 3, 'Texas': 3,\n        'Oklahoma': 3,\n        # West\n        'Montana': 4, 'Idaho': 4, 'Wyoming': 4, 'Colorado': 4, 'New Mexico': 4,\n        'Arizona': 4, 'Utah': 4, 'Nevada': 4, 'Washington': 4, 'Oregon': 4,\n        'California': 4, 'Alaska': 4, 'Hawaii': 4,\n        'Unknown': 2  # Default to Midwest for unknown\n    }\n    result_df['REGION_CODE'] = df['Q-demos-state'].map(state_to_region).fillna(2)\n\n    # ============================================================================\n    # AMAZON USAGE FEATURES\n    # ============================================================================\n\n    # Number of Amazon accounts\n    result_df['AMAZON_ACCOUNTS'] = df['Q-amazon-use-howmany'].fillna(df['Q-amazon-use-howmany'].median())\n\n    # Household size\n    result_df['HOUSEHOLD_SIZE'] = df['Q-amazon-use-hh-size'].fillna(df['Q-amazon-use-hh-size'].median())\n\n    # Amazon frequency: Ordinal encoding\n    frequency_mapping = {\n        'Less than 5 times per month': 2,\n        '5 - 10 times per month': 7.5,\n        'More than 10 times per month': 15,\n        'Unknown': 7\n    }\n    result_df['AMAZON_FREQUENCY'] = df['Q-amazon-use-how-oft'].map(frequency_mapping).fillna(7)\n\n    # ============================================================================\n    # HEALTH & LIFESTYLE FEATURES\n    # ============================================================================\n\n    # Substance use features (already binary)\n    result_df['CIGARETTE_USE'] = df['Q-substance-use-cigarettes'].fillna(0).astype(int)\n    result_df['MARIJUANA_USE'] = df['Q-substance-use-marijuana'].fillna(0).astype(int)\n    result_df['ALCOHOL_USE'] = df['Q-substance-use-alcohol'].fillna(0).astype(int)\n\n    # Health conditions\n    result_df['DIABETES'] = df['Q-personal-diabetes'].fillna(0).astype(int)\n    result_df['WHEELCHAIR_USE'] = df['Q-personal-wheelchair'].fillna(0).astype(int)\n\n    # ============================================================================\n    # ENGINEERED INTERACTION FEATURES\n    # ============================================================================\n\n    # High income + high education = higher SES indicator\n    result_df['HIGH_SES_SCORE'] = (result_df['EDUCATION_LEVEL'] + result_df['INCOME_LEVEL']) / 2\n\n    # Heavy Amazon user + large household\n    result_df['HEAVY_AMAZON_USER'] = ((result_df['AMAZON_FREQUENCY'] > 10).astype(int))\n    result_df['HOUSEHOLD_AMAZON_INTENSITY'] = result_df['HOUSEHOLD_SIZE'] * result_df['HEAVY_AMAZON_USER']\n\n    # Health condition indicator (has at least one mentioned)\n    result_df['HAS_HEALTH_CONDITION'] = (\n        (result_df['DIABETES'] == 1) | (result_df['WHEELCHAIR_USE'] == 1)\n    ).astype(int)\n\n    # ============================================================================\n    # VALIDATE ALL FEATURES ARE NUMERIC\n    # ============================================================================\n\n    # Check data types\n    non_numeric = result_df.select_dtypes(exclude=['int64', 'int32', 'float64', 'float32']).columns.tolist()\n    if non_numeric and non_numeric != ['Survey ResponseID']:\n        print(f\"Warning: Non-numeric columns found: {non_numeric}\")\n\n    # Fill any remaining NaN values\n    numeric_cols = result_df.select_dtypes(include=['float64', 'int64']).columns\n    for col in numeric_cols:\n        result_df[col] = result_df[col].fillna(result_df[col].median())\n\n    # Remove constant features (features with zero variance)\n    variance_cols = {}\n    for col in numeric_cols:\n        if col != 'Survey ResponseID':\n            variance_cols[col] = result_df[col].var()\n\n    # Keep features with non-zero variance\n    low_variance_threshold = 0.001\n    low_variance_features = [col for col, var in variance_cols.items() if var < low_variance_threshold]\n    if low_variance_features:\n        print(f\"Removing low-variance features: {low_variance_features}\")\n        result_df = result_df.drop(columns=low_variance_features)\n\n    print(f\"\\nFinal shape: {result_df.shape}\")\n    print(f\"Number of engineered features: {result_df.shape[1] - 1}\")  # Keep Survey ResponseID for merging later\n    result_df = df[['Survey ResponseID']].copy()\n\n    # ============================================================================\n    # DEMOGRAPHIC FEATURES\n    # ============================================================================\n\n    # Age: Convert to numeric (take midpoint of range)\n    age_mapping = {\n        '18 - 24 years': 21,\n        '25 - 34 years': 29.5,\n        '35 - 44 years': 39.5,\n        '45 - 54 years': 49.5,\n        '55 - 64 years': 59.5,\n        '65 and older': 70,\n        'Unknown': np.nan\n    }\n    result_df['AGE_NUMERIC'] = df['Q-demos-age'].map(age_mapping)\n    result_df['AGE_NUMERIC'] = result_df['AGE_NUMERIC'].fillna(result_df['AGE_NUMERIC'].median())\n\n    # Gender: Binary encoding\n    gender_mapping = {'Male': 1, 'Female': 0, 'Unknown': np.nan}\n    result_df['GENDER_MALE'] = df['Q-demos-gender'].map(gender_mapping).fillna(0)\n\n    # Hispanic: Binary\n    result_df['HISPANIC'] = df['Q-demos-hispanic'].map({'Yes': 1, 'No': 0, 'Unknown': 0}).fillna(0)\n\n    # Race: One-hot encode main categories (consolidate Unknown)\n    race_categories = df['Q-demos-race'].fillna('Unknown').unique()\n    print(f\"Race categories: {race_categories}\")\n\n    # Create dummy variables for race\n    race_df = pd.get_dummies(df['Q-demos-race'].fillna('Unknown'), prefix='RACE')\n    for col in race_df.columns:\n        result_df[col] = race_df[col].astype(int)\n\n    # Education: Ordinal encoding (higher education = higher value)\n    education_mapping = {\n        'Some high school or less': 1,\n        'High school diploma or GED': 2,\n        \"Some college or Associate's degree\": 3,\n        \"Bachelor's degree\": 4,\n        'Graduate or professional degree (MA, MS, MBA, PhD, JD, MD, DDS, etc)': 5,\n        'Prefer not to say': 2.5,\n        'Unknown': 2\n    }\n    result_df['EDUCATION_LEVEL'] = df['Q-demos-education'].map(education_mapping).fillna(2)\n\n    # Income: Ordinal encoding (higher income = higher value)\n    income_mapping = {\n        'Less than $25,000': 1,\n        '$25,000 - $49,999': 2,\n        '$50,000 - $74,999': 3,\n        '$75,000 - $99,999': 4,\n        '$100,000 - $149,999': 5,\n        '$150,000 or more': 6,\n        'Prefer not to say': 3.5,\n        'Unknown': 3\n    }\n    result_df['INCOME_LEVEL'] = df['Q-demos-income'].map(income_mapping).fillna(3)\n\n    # Sexual orientation: Binary (LGBTQ+ vs non-LGBTQ)\n    result_df['LGBTQ_IDENTITY'] = ((df['Q-sexual-orientation'] == 'LGBTQ+').astype(int)).fillna(0)\n\n    # State: Encode as region codes (Northeast, Southeast, Midwest, Southwest, West)\n    state_to_region = {\n        # Northeast\n        'Maine': 1, 'New Hampshire': 1, 'Vermont': 1, 'Massachusetts': 1, 'Rhode Island': 1,\n        'Connecticut': 1, 'New York': 1, 'New Jersey': 1, 'Pennsylvania': 1,\n        # Midwest\n        'Ohio': 2, 'Indiana': 2, 'Illinois': 2, 'Michigan': 2, 'Wisconsin': 2,\n        'Minnesota': 2, 'Iowa': 2, 'Missouri': 2, 'North Dakota': 2, 'South Dakota': 2,\n        'Nebraska': 2, 'Kansas': 2,\n        # South\n        'Delaware': 3, 'Maryland': 3, 'Virginia': 3, 'West Virginia': 3, 'Kentucky': 3,\n        'Tennessee': 3, 'North Carolina': 3, 'South Carolina': 3, 'Georgia': 3, 'Florida': 3,\n        'Alabama': 3, 'Mississippi': 3, 'Louisiana': 3, 'Arkansas': 3, 'Texas': 3,\n        'Oklahoma': 3,\n        # West\n        'Montana': 4, 'Idaho': 4, 'Wyoming': 4, 'Colorado': 4, 'New Mexico': 4,\n        'Arizona': 4, 'Utah': 4, 'Nevada': 4, 'Washington': 4, 'Oregon': 4,\n        'California': 4, 'Alaska': 4, 'Hawaii': 4,\n        'Unknown': 2  # Default to Midwest for unknown\n    }\n    result_df['REGION_CODE'] = df['Q-demos-state'].map(state_to_region).fillna(2)\n\n    # ============================================================================\n    # AMAZON USAGE FEATURES\n    # ============================================================================\n\n    # Number of Amazon accounts\n    result_df['AMAZON_ACCOUNTS'] = df['Q-amazon-use-howmany'].fillna(df['Q-amazon-use-howmany'].median())\n\n    # Household size\n    result_df['HOUSEHOLD_SIZE'] = df['Q-amazon-use-hh-size'].fillna(df['Q-amazon-use-hh-size'].median())\n\n    # Amazon frequency: Ordinal encoding\n    frequency_mapping = {\n        'Less than 5 times per month': 2,\n        '5 - 10 times per month': 7.5,\n        'More than 10 times per month': 15,\n        'Unknown': 7\n    }\n    result_df['AMAZON_FREQUENCY'] = df['Q-amazon-use-how-oft'].map(frequency_mapping).fillna(7)\n\n    # ============================================================================\n    # HEALTH & LIFESTYLE FEATURES\n    # ============================================================================\n\n    # Substance use features (already binary)\n    result_df['CIGARETTE_USE'] = df['Q-substance-use-cigarettes'].fillna(0).astype(int)\n    result_df['MARIJUANA_USE'] = df['Q-substance-use-marijuana'].fillna(0).astype(int)\n    result_df['ALCOHOL_USE'] = df['Q-substance-use-alcohol'].fillna(0).astype(int)\n\n    # Health conditions\n    result_df['DIABETES'] = df['Q-personal-diabetes'].fillna(0).astype(int)\n    result_df['WHEELCHAIR_USE'] = df['Q-personal-wheelchair'].fillna(0).astype(int)\n\n    # ============================================================================\n    # ENGINEERED INTERACTION FEATURES\n    # ============================================================================\n\n    # High income + high education = higher SES indicator\n    result_df['HIGH_SES_SCORE'] = (result_df['EDUCATION_LEVEL'] + result_df['INCOME_LEVEL']) / 2\n\n    # Heavy Amazon user + large household\n    result_df['HEAVY_AMAZON_USER'] = ((result_df['AMAZON_FREQUENCY'] > 10).astype(int))\n    result_df['HOUSEHOLD_AMAZON_INTENSITY'] = result_df['HOUSEHOLD_SIZE'] * result_df['HEAVY_AMAZON_USER']\n\n    # Health condition indicator (has at least one mentioned)\n    result_df['HAS_HEALTH_CONDITION'] = (\n        (result_df['DIABETES'] == 1) | (result_df['WHEELCHAIR_USE'] == 1)\n    ).astype(int)\n\n    # ============================================================================\n    # VALIDATE ALL FEATURES ARE NUMERIC\n    # ============================================================================\n\n    # Check data types\n    non_numeric = result_df.select_dtypes(exclude=['int64', 'int32', 'float64', 'float32']).columns.tolist()\n    if non_numeric and non_numeric != ['Survey ResponseID']:\n        print(f\"Warning: Non-numeric columns found: {non_numeric}\")\n\n    # Fill any remaining NaN values\n    numeric_cols = result_df.select_dtypes(include=['float64', 'int64']).columns\n    for col in numeric_cols:\n        result_df[col] = result_df[col].fillna(result_df[col].median())\n\n    # Remove constant features (features with zero variance)\n    variance_cols = {}\n    for col in numeric_cols:\n        if col != 'Survey ResponseID':\n            variance_cols[col] = result_df[col].var()\n\n    # Keep Survey ResponseID for merging later\n    result_df = df[['Survey ResponseID']].copy()\n\n    # ============================================================================\n    # DEMOGRAPHIC FEATURES\n    # Education: Ordinal encoding (higher education = higher value)\n    education_mapping = {\n        'Some high school or less': 1,\n        'High school diploma or GED': 2,\n        \"Some college or Associate's degree\": 3,\n        \"Bachelor's degree\": 4,\n        'Graduate or professional degree (MA, MS, MBA, PhD, JD, MD, DDS, etc)': 5,\n        'Prefer not to say': 2.5,\n        'Unknown': 2\n    }\n    result_df['EDUCATION_LEVEL'] = df['Q-demos-education'].map(education_mapping).fillna(2)\n\n    # Default to Midwest for unknown\n    }\n    result_df['REGION_CODE'] = df['Q-demos-state'].map(state_to_region).fillna(2)\n\n    # ============================================================================\n    # Household size\n    result_df['HOUSEHOLD_SIZE'] = df['Q-amazon-use-hh-size'].fillna(df['Q-amazon-use-hh-size'].median())\n\n    # Amazon frequency: Ordinal encoding\n    frequency_mapping = {\n        'Less than 5 times per month': 2,\n        '5 - 10 times per month': 7.5,\n        'More than 10 times per month': 15,\n        'Unknown': 7\n    }\n    result_df['AMAZON_FREQUENCY'] = df['Q-amazon-use-how-oft'].map(frequency_mapping).fillna(7)\n\n    # ============================================================================\n    # HEALTH & LIFESTYLE FEATURES\n    # ============================================================================\n\n    # Substance use features - Convert Yes/No to 1/0\n    result_df['CIGARETTE_USE'] = df['Q-substance-use-cigarettes'].map({'Yes': 1, 'No': 0}).fillna(0).astype(int)\n    result_df['MARIJUANA_USE'] = df['Q-substance-use-marijuana'].map({'Yes': 1, 'No': 0}).fillna(0).astype(int)\n    result_df['ALCOHOL_USE'] = df['Q-substance-use-alcohol'].map({'Yes': 1, 'No': 0}).fillna(0).astype(int)\n\n    # Health conditions - Convert Yes/No to 1/0\n    result_df['DIABETES'] = df['Q-personal-diabetes'].map({'Yes': 1, 'No': 0}).fillna(0).astype(int)\n    result_df['WHEELCHAIR_USE'] = df['Q-personal-wheelchair'].map({'Yes': 1, 'No': 0}).fillna(0).astype(int)\n\n    # Check data types\n    non_numeric = result_df.select_dtypes(exclude=['int64', 'int32', 'float64', 'float32']).columns.tolist()\n    if non_numeric and non_numeric != ['Survey ResponseID']:\n        print(f\"Warning: Non-numeric columns found: {non_numeric}\")\n\n    # Merge with survey features\n    data_intensive_merged = data_intensive.merge(survey_df, on='Survey ResponseID', how='left')\n    data_extensive_merged = data_extensive.merge(survey_df, on='Survey ResponseID', how='left')\n\n    # Get feature columns (all numeric columns except ID and control variables)\n    feature_cols = [col for col in survey_df.columns if col != 'Survey ResponseID']\n\n    # Create treatment indicator\n    data_intensive_merged['Treated'] = data_intensive_merged['Period']\n    data_extensive_merged['Treated'] = data_extensive_merged['Period']\n\n    results_intensive = []\n    results_extensive = []\n\n    print(f\"\\n{'='*80}\")\n    print(f\"Analyzing {group_label} | Categories: {len(category_group)}\")\n    print(f\"{'='*80}\")\n\n    # Intensive margin: DiD regression on spending\n    print(f\"\\nIntensive Margin Analysis (Spending Per User):\")\n    print(f\"N observations: {len(data_intensive_merged)}, N users: {data_intensive_merged['Survey ResponseID'].nunique()}\")\n\n    for feature in feature_cols:\n        try:\n            # Skip if feature has no variance\n            if data_intensive_merged[feature].std() == 0:\n                continue\n\n            # Create interaction term\n            data_intensive_merged[f'{feature}_X_Treated'] = data_intensive_merged[feature] * data_intensive_merged['Treated']\n\n            # Simple DiD: Total_Spend ~ Treated + Feature + Feature*Treated\n            X = data_intensive_merged[[f'{feature}_X_Treated']].copy()\n            X['const'] = 1\n            y = data_intensive_merged['Total_Spend'].values\n\n            # Only include observations with valid data\n            valid_idx = X.notna().all(axis=1) & (y.notna())\n            X_valid = X[valid_idx]\n            y_valid = y[valid_idx]\n\n            if len(X_valid) < 10:\n                continue\n\n            model = OLS(y_valid, X_valid)\n            result = model.fit()\n\n            did_coef = result.params[f'{feature}_X_Treated']\n            pvalue = result.pvalues[f'{feature}_X_Treated']\n\n            results_intensive.append({\n                'feature': feature,\n                'did_estimate': float(did_coef),\n                'p_value': float(pvalue),\n                'method': 'Univariate DiD'\n            })\n\n        except Exception as e:\n            continue\n\n    #
